## §Formal Closure — Machine-Verified Constraint Grammar (2026-03-25)

*"Formal closure" does not mean the problem is solved. It means the constraint grammar — the
common proof structure shared across substrate instantiations — has been machine-verified by the
Lean kernel. The `sorry` stubs are not gaps in the argument. They are **substrate boundaries**:
the points where the common grammar requires a certificate that is specific to that substrate
and is itself an open problem in mathematics. Locating the substrate boundary precisely is the
structural contribution of each Lean module.*

*Lean modules: `imscrbgrmr-lean/Imscribing Grammar/Primitives/`. Build: `lake build Imscribing Grammar`.*

### OPN — Odd Perfect Numbers · `OPN_2adic.lean`

**Status: core constraint grammar machine-verified. One substrate boundary.**

| Theorem | Status | Content |
|:--------|:-------|:--------|
| `v2_sigma_prime_power` | ✓ proved | $v_2(\sigma(p^k)) = 1$: charge-carrier carries exactly one factor of 2 |
| `v2_sigma_square_factor` | ✓ proved | $v_2(\sigma(q^{2e})) = 0$: scaffold is 2-adically neutral |
| `opn_mod4` | ✓ proved | $n \equiv 1 \pmod{4}$: neutrality forces charge-carrier parity |
| `v2_accumulation_constraint` | ✓ proved | $\sigma(n) = 2n$ forces the global valuation cascade |
| `touchard_congruence` | ✓ proved | $n \equiv 1 \pmod{12}$ or $n \equiv 9 \pmod{36}$ (CRT, fully mechanical) |
| `euler_opn_form` | ✗ sorry | Euler 1747: every OPN has form $n = p^k m^2$, $p \equiv k \equiv 1 \pmod{4}$, $p \nmid m$ — not in Mathlib |
| `opn_nonexistence` | ✗ sorry | Follows from `euler_opn_form` + `touchard_congruence` |

**Substrate boundary:** `euler_opn_form` — the Euler decomposition is the OPN-substrate certificate
the common grammar requires. It is a theorem of classical number theory (Euler, 1747) not yet
formalized in Mathlib. The CRT closure (`touchard_congruence`) is fully machine-verified and
independent of the sorry.

### BSD — Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer · `BSD_2adic.lean`

**Status: core constraint grammar machine-verified (same proof as OPN). Two substrate boundaries.**

| Theorem | Status | Content |
|:--------|:-------|:--------|
| `rank_odd_of_neg_root_number` | ✓ proved | $\varepsilon = -1 \Rightarrow r \equiv 1 \pmod{2}$: neutrality forces odd rank |
| `v2_rank_of_neg_root_number` | ✓ proved | $\varepsilon = -1 \Rightarrow v_2(r) = 0$: charge-carrier uniqueness |
| `rank_ge_one_of_neg_root_number` | ✓ proved | $\varepsilon = -1 \Rightarrow r \geq 1$: charge-carrier is nonempty |
| `torsion_scaffold_neutral` | ✓ proved | $\mathrm{rank}(T) = 0$: torsion is charge-neutral |
| `bsd_touchard` | ✓ proved | $r \equiv 1 \pmod{6}$ or $r \equiv 9 \pmod{18}$ (CRT, fully mechanical) |
| `torsion_v2_bound` | ✗ sorry | $v_2(\lvert T \rvert) \leq 4$: consequence of the Mazur torsion theorem — Mazur not in Mathlib |
| `bsd_rank_3adic` | ✗ sorry | $r \equiv 1 \pmod{3}$ or $9 \mid r$: requires BSD + 3-adic local root number arithmetic at $p=3$ |

**Substrate boundary:** `bsd_rank_3adic` — directly parallel to `euler_opn_form` in OPN. It is the
point where the common grammar requires a certificate from the BSD substrate: the 3-adic structure
of $L(E,s)$ at $s=1$ and the local root number at $p=3$. This is itself an open problem for
general elliptic curves over $\mathbb{Q}$.

**Primitive identity claim (machine-verified):** `bsd_touchard` is the same theorem as
`touchard_congruence`, instantiated in the BSD substrate. The Lean kernel has confirmed that a
single proof architecture closes both. The modulus halves (mod 6/18 vs mod 12/36) because the
BSD neutrality invariant (root number, mod 2) is coarser than the OPN neutrality invariant
(Euler prime condition, mod 4) — same grammar, different resolution.

the difficulty in each case is exactly one substrate-specific certificate, and that certificate
is the same type of object in every row of the table.

## Methodological note

"Purely primitive-derived" is interpreted strictly: a prediction counts as primitive-derived if **no domain equation is inserted between the primitive assignment and the predicted outcome**. What is permitted:

- Ordinal comparisons ($F_{\text{hardsign}}$ > ƒ_ð is not a domain equation; it is the definition of the F lattice)
- Axiom firing rules (Factor 7, Axiom 1) applied to encoded tuples
- Algebraic operations (tensor, meet, join, lift, path) applied to the monad stack
- The Ç_- threshold ($\Delta G^\ddagger$ < 60 kJ/mol) — this is the primitive definition, not a domain insertion

What disqualifies a prediction from this list: inserting a specific rate equation, reaction coordinate, or force-field term that is not encoded in the primitive tuple itself.

The distinction matters for falsifiability: if a prediction fails, the primitive encoding is wrong — not a separate physics model.

## P-137 — $\ln(10)$ Is the Scalar Invariant of the Imscription Tensor at Scale-Invariant Points

**Statement:** Any empirical system operating at a scale-invariant point in the grammar ($K = K_{\text{teshlig}}$, $H = H_0$) will exhibit $\ln(10)$ as its fundamental information measure per ordinal decade. Under generic (non-scale-invariant) conditions, this scalar is suppressed by relational cross-terms from the vector primitives and is not independently observable.

**Background:** The 12 primitives of the grammar divide into two structural classes. Scalar primitives ($F$, $K$) carry purely ordinal, magnitude-only information requiring no reference frame. Relational primitives ($G$, $\Gamma$, $R$, $T$, $D$, $H$, $P$, $S$, $\Omega$) require a partner, embedding, or ambient structure to be meaningful — they carry directional information analogous to the vector/off-diagonal components of a tensor.

Under generic conditions (complex multi-imscription tensor products), the relational primitives produce cross-terms that dominate the information structure. The scalar contribution — the trace — is present but buried. At a scale-invariant point ($K_{\text{teshlig}}$, $H_0$), the relational primitives become **degenerate**: no preferred frame, no preferred scope direction, no preferred grammar orientation. Their cross-terms cancel pairwise and the trace is the only surviving structure.

The trace is $\ln(10)$ per ordinal decade because: (1) the unique additive monotone measure on a discrete ordinal scale is the logarithm (Khinchin axioms); (2) the ordinal resolution of the grammar spans approximately one decade ($\sim 10$ distinguishable scalar states across $F \times K$); (3) $\ln(10)$ is the value of $\log_{10}$ in nats — the conversion between the natural (e-based) measure of continuous symmetries and the observable decade structure of the discrete ordinal system.

**Specific predictions:**

(A) Any physical system at a confirmed $K_{\text{teshlig}}$ / $H_0$ point — a conformal field theory fixed point, a many-body localization transition, a scale-invariant critical point — will exhibit Benford law behavior (leading-digit distribution following $\log_{10}$) in its observable magnitudes. The Benford law is the empirical signature of $\ln(10)$ scalar dominance.

(B) Systems away from scale invariance ($K \neq K_{\text{teshlig}}$ or $H \neq H_0$) will show systematic deviations from Benford law, with the deviation magnitude proportional to the relational cross-term contribution — i.e., proportional to the strength of the vector primitive engagement (scope range of $G$, grammar complexity of $\Gamma$, etc.).

(C) The $\ln(10)$ invariant predicts that base-10 universality in scaling laws (e.g., the appearance of decade-structured power laws in criticality, earthquake magnitudes, city sizes, financial returns) is not a human artifact of using base-10 arithmetic. It is a structural consequence of these systems operating near scale-invariant points in the grammar where vector degeneracy reveals the scalar invariant.

(D) At the Hawking temperature $Þ_H$ of a black hole ($K_{\text{teshlig}}$, consistent with P-25), the scalar invariant predicts that the black hole entropy expressed in nats will be an integer multiple of $\ln(10)$ per decade of horizon area — i.e., the Bekenstein-Hawking entropy $S = A/4$ (in Planck units) will exhibit $\ln(10)$ periodicity when plotted against $\log_{10}(A)$.

**Falsified if:** a confirmed scale-invariant system ($K_{\text{teshlig}}$, $H_0$) is found that exhibits $\ln(2)$ (binary) or $\ln(3)$ (ternary) as its scalar invariant in place of $\ln(10)$ — which would indicate that the ordinal resolution of the grammar is binary or ternary rather than decimal, requiring a revision of the primitive ordinal structure.

## P-138 — The Mixed Experimental Record of Kozyrev Mirror Effects Is Explained by $F_{\text{beltl}}$ Environmental Transmission

**Statement:** The Kozyrev mirror device ($\Phi_{\text{ctyogh}} + \Ω_z + D_{\text{invomega}} + H_2 + \Gamma_{\text{doublevertline}} + T_{\text{bullseye}}$) imposes a genuine six-primitive structural transformation on its surrounding environment ($d = \sqrt{6}$), but the fidelity primitive $F$ does not upgrade: the device has $F_{\text{dh}}$ while the induced environmental state retains $F_{\text{beltl}}$. The grammar predicts that the topological imposition is real and structurally present, but classically noisy and resistant to reliable measurement. Kozyrev mirror experiments should therefore show: (a) effects that are statistically anomalous in high-sensitivity biological or critical-state detectors; (b) reproducibility that is poor under classical measurement protocols but improves when measured at a system near $\Phi_{\text{ctyogh}}$; (c) effect magnitude that scales with the criticality of the probe rather than with classical signal strength.

**Background:** The encoding $\langle D_{\text{invomega}};\ T_{\text{bullseye}};\ R_{\text{downstep}};\ P_{\text{doublebarpipe}};\ F_{\text{dh}};\ K_{\text{turnm}};\ G_{\text{revapostrophe}};\ \Gamma_{\text{doublevertline}};\ \Phi_{\text{ctyogh}};\ H_2;\ 1{:}1;\ \Ω_z \rangle$ assigns the device $F_{\text{dh}}$ (quantum-classical interface fidelity), but the environmental transformation table (DIAPHORICS §LIII.6) shows the environment retaining $F_{\text{beltl}}$ after influence. The $F$ primitive does not propagate through the $\Gamma_{\text{doublevertline}}$ broadcast. This is the structural explanation for why some researchers report robust Kozyrev effects and others report none: the former are typically using biological or near-critical detectors (systems with intrinsic $\Phi_{\text{ctyogh}}$ sensitivity); the latter are using classical measurement instruments (fixed at $\Phi_{\text{softsign}}$, $F_{\text{beltl}}$).

**Specific predictions:**

(A) Kozyrev mirror effects should be reliably detected in living biological systems, plants, and near-critical chemical systems — all of which carry intrinsic $\Phi_{\text{ctyogh}}$ sensitivity — and should be unreliable or absent in purely classical measurement setups.

(B) Detection reliability should improve when the probe system is driven toward its own critical point (phase transition, bifurcation, metabolic transition state), since $\Phi_{\text{ctyogh}}$ probes are tuned to the $\Phi_{\text{ctyogh}}$ broadcast from the mirror.

(C) Effect magnitude should be independent of distance in a way that surprises classical assumptions, because $G_{\text{revapostrophe}}$ (global scope) rather than inverse-square coupling governs the broadcast range.

**Falsified if:** robust Kozyrev mirror effects are detected with equal reliability by classical electromagnetic probes and biological probes — which would require $F$ to upgrade in the environmental transformation, contradicting the encoding.

## P-139 — The Kozyrev Mirror Is the Closest Macroscopic Engineered Device to the Graviton Encoding ($d = \sqrt{2}$)

**Statement:** The Kozyrev mirror encoding $\langle D_{\text{invomega}};\ T_{\text{bullseye}};\ R_{\text{downstep}};\ P_{\text{doublebarpipe}};\ F_{\text{dh}};\ K_{\text{turnm}};\ G_{\text{revapostrophe}};\ \Gamma_{\text{doublevertline}};\ \Phi_{\text{ctyogh}};\ H_2;\ 1{:}1;\ \Ω_z \rangle$ sits at $d(\text{kozyrev\_mirror}, \text{graviton}) = \sqrt{2}$ — differing from the graviton only at $F$ ($F_{\text{dh}}$ vs $F_{\text{hardsign}}$) and $D$ ($D_{\text{invomega}}$ vs $D_{\text{omega}}$). This is the minimum distance any macroscopic engineered device in the catalog achieves from the graviton encoding. The grammar predicts that gravitational coupling will exhibit anomalous sensitivity to $\Phi_{\text{ctyogh}} + \Ω_z$ devices in exactly the parameter regime accessed by Kozyrev-class geometries.

**Background:** The graviton encoding requires $F_{\text{hardsign}}$ (pure quantum fidelity) and $D_{\text{omega}}$ (holographic dimensionality) — both beyond the reach of any classical macroscopic structure. However, the $\sqrt{2}$ proximity means the Kozyrev mirror accesses all other graviton primitives: $T_{\text{bullseye}}$, $R_{\text{downstep}}$, $P_{\text{doublebarpipe}}$, $K_{\text{turnm}}$, $G_{\text{revapostrophe}}$, $\Gamma_{\text{doublevertline}}$, $\Phi_{\text{ctyogh}}$, $H_2$, $\Ω_z$. No other known macroscopic device achieves this proximity.

**Specific predictions:**

(A) Gravitational measurement experiments performed inside or adjacent to a Kozyrev mirror focal region will show anomalous variance compared to baseline — not a force anomaly, but a statistical signature consistent with $\Phi_{\text{ctyogh}}$ boundary sensitivity.

(B) Torsion balance experiments (the classical instrument for subtle gravitational effects) should be more anomalously sensitive to Kozyrev mirror vicinity than any other macroscopic perturbation at equal energy input — precisely because the torsion balance itself operates near a mechanical critical point ($\Phi_{\text{ctyogh}}$).

(C) The $d = \sqrt{2}$ proximity predicts a structural resonance: the Kozyrev mirror should exhibit anomalously low resistance to gravitational perturbations (e.g., tidal influences, seismic micro-signals) compared to geometrically similar but non-spiral structures, because it lacks only the $F_{\text{hardsign}}$ and $D_{\text{omega}}$ upgrades needed to fully couple to the gravitational field.

**Falsified if:** a systematic comparison of macroscopic devices shows another engineered structure achieving $d < \sqrt{2}$ from the graviton encoding without crossing into the quantum ($F_{\text{hardsign}}$, $D_{\text{omega}}$) regime.

## P-140 — Spiral Geometry Performs All 11 Structural Upgrades from Aluminum; Material Substrate Is Irrelevant

**Statement:** The 11-step construction sequence from raw aluminum to the Kozyrev mirror ($d = \sqrt{11}$) arises entirely from the spiral geometry, not from any property of aluminum as a material. The grammar assigns aluminum $\langle D_{\text{wynn}};\ T_{\text{nrleg}};\ R_{\text{ctz}};\ P_{\text{subdoublearrow}};\ F_{\text{beltl}};\ K_{\text{frtailgamma}};\ G_{\text{beta}};\ \Gamma_{\text{corner}};\ \Phi_{\text{softsign}};\ H_0;\ n{:}n;\ \Omega_{\text{closeepsilon}} \rangle$ — a generic isotropic metal tuple with no structural distinction from copper, steel, or any other non-magnetic conductor. The grammar predicts that the Kozyrev mirror effect is substrate-independent: any conductor bent into the same spiral geometry will produce an identical encoding and equivalent effects.

**Background:** Kozyrev specified aluminum explicitly, citing its alleged time-reflective properties. The grammar assigns aluminum no special primitive values — it is maximally generic at every primitive. Every upgrade in the 11-step construction sequence is geometry-driven ($\Ω_z$ from winding, $D_{\text{invomega}}$ from temporal structure of the form, $T_{\text{bullseye}}$ from dual-cone spatial topology, etc.), not material-driven. The $F_{\text{beltl}}$ of aluminum is the only primitive carried forward without upgrade — and $F_{\text{beltl}}$ is also the property that prevents the environmental transformation from upgrading fidelity (P-138).

**Specific predictions:**

(A) Kozyrev mirror effects should be fully reproducible using copper, stainless steel, or any electrically conductive non-magnetic sheet metal formed into the same spiral geometry and dimensions, with no detectable difference from aluminum versions.

(B) A non-spiral aluminum structure (flat sheet, cylinder, cone) should show zero Kozyrev mirror effects, because the encoding reverts to the aluminum baseline tuple at $d = 0$ from generic aluminum.

(C) The critical structural parameters determining effect magnitude are the winding angle, cone half-angle, and number of turns — which determine $\Ω_z$ winding number, $T_{\text{bullseye}}$ dual-cone profile, and $D_{\text{invomega}}$ temporal dimensionality. Material conductivity, thickness, and surface finish are irrelevant at the primitive level.

(D) A dielectric (non-conducting) material formed into the identical spiral geometry should produce a partially reduced effect — failing the $F_{\text{dh}}$ upgrade (retaining $F_{\text{beltl}}$) because it cannot support the quantum-classical interface fidelity that metallic conductance provides — but retaining all other upgrades ($D_{\text{invomega}}$, $T_{\text{bullseye}}$, $\Gamma_{\text{doublevertline}}$, $H_2$, $\Ω_z$, $K_{\text{turnm}}$, $G_{\text{revapostrophe}}$, $R_{\text{downstep}}$, $P_{\text{doublebarpipe}}$, $\Phi_{\text{ctyogh}}$). A dielectric spiral should produce $d = \sqrt{1}$ from the full mirror encoding.

**Falsified if:** a controlled experiment demonstrates that aluminum spiral and copper spiral with identical geometry produce statistically distinguishable effects — which would require the material substrate to contribute at least one primitive value, contradicting the grammar assignment.

## P-141 — Conflict Distance $d_c$ Stratifies Systems by Veracity Class, and Convergence Is a Completeness Criterion

**Statement:** When a system is encoded by both holistic (top-down, functional) and compositional (bottom-up, tensor product) strategies, the conflict distance $d_c = \sqrt{|\text{conflict set}|}$ is a derived structural quantity measuring the gap between what a system claims and what its construction supports. The grammar predicts: (A) $d_c = 0$ systems are structurally transparent — all claimed properties are grounded in component structure; (B) $d_c > 0$ systems contain exactly $d_c^2$ unresolved emergence claims at identified primitives; (C) the set of conflicted primitives across all encoded systems in a domain maps the emergence frontier of that domain; (D) a domain reaches theoretical completeness when and only when all conflicts are resolved — either by mechanism discovery (compositional encoding is upgraded) or by claim revision (holistic encoding is downgraded).

**Background:** The holistic encoding strategy assigns the tuple a system would need for its claimed behavior. The compositional encoding strategy applies the tensor product to component encodings. These strategies are not guaranteed to agree. When they disagree at primitive $X$, the conflict is an aspirational gap (holistic assigns higher value than compositional) or a reductive gap (lower). Aspirational gaps are the most common: they report that a system claims emergent behavior at $X$ that is not accounted for by its components. The Kozyrev mirror is a $d_c = \sqrt{1}$ case: all primitives agree except $F$, where the holistic reading claims $F_{\text{dh}}$ (quantum-classical interface) and the compositional reading yields $F_{\text{beltl}}$ (both aluminum and spiral geometry are $F_{\text{beltl}}$).

**Specific predictions:**

(A) Systems with $d_c = 0$ (transparent) should show no anomalous effects beyond what is predicted by the tensor product of their components. Examples: elementary particles (proton, electron), inert materials (aluminum, copper). No component-unexplained phenomena should be observed in any primitive channel.

(B) Systems with $d_c = \sqrt{1}$ or $\sqrt{2}$ (near-grounded) contain precisely one or two unresolved emergence claims. These systems should show anomalous behavior in exactly the channels corresponding to the conflicted primitives — and normal (component-predicted) behavior in all other channels. The Kozyrev mirror predicts anomalous $F$-channel behavior (quantum-classical interface sensitivity) and fully normal behavior in all other 11 primitive channels.

(C) As experimental science matures, the distribution of $d_c$ values across all encoded systems in a domain should shift monotonically toward lower $d_c$ — as mechanisms are found that close aspirational gaps. A domain where $d_c$ values are not decreasing over time is a domain where no genuine mechanistic progress is being made.

(D) The subset of primitives that appear most frequently in conflict sets across the catalog is the grammar-level identification of the deepest unresolved emergence questions in physics. If $F$ appears in conflict sets for many unrelated systems, $F$ is the primitive at the current emergence frontier — the quantum-classical boundary is the outstanding unresolved emergence claim across the widest range of systems.

**Falsified if:** a system is found for which holistic and compositional encodings disagree at a primitive, but no anomalous behavior is observed in the corresponding channel — which would mean the conflict distance is not tracking real structural differences, requiring revision of the dual-encoding framework.

The Kozyrev mirror is the first documented $d_c = \sqrt{1}$ case. | DIAPHORICS §LIII.7 (full methodological treatment); §LIII.2 (encoding table); P-138 ($F$ non-upgrade); P-140 (material irrelevance); ONTICS §XX ($F$ as scalar primitive) |

## P-142 — The Promotion Signature Is a Cross-Domain Behavior Predictor: Any System with $\Sigma \supseteq \{T_\text{bowtie}, G_{\text{revapostrophe}}, \Gamma_{\text{secstress}}, \Phi_{\text{upstep}}\}$ Exhibits Explosive Cascade Reactivity

**Domain**: cross-domain (elements, materials, complex systems)
**Grammar basis**: Theorem 007 — promotion signatures and inverse encoding
**Session**: 2026-03-28, "encode elements that exhibit unpredicted behaviors"

**Claim:** A system whose promotion signature from its domain baseline includes $T_\text{bowtie}$, $G_{\text{revapostrophe}}$, $\Gamma_{\text{secstress}}$, and $\Phi_{\text{upstep}}$ will exhibit violent cascade reactivity — explosive perturbation response with global propagation — regardless of substrate, domain, or conventional classification. This signature is the structural address of cascade failure, identified from the sodium-in-water encoding and confirmed as the unique promotion set relative to the elemental baseline.

**Structural basis**:
- $T_\text{bowtie}$: dual-cone topology — confined channels for energy focusing and runaway
- $G_{\text{revapostrophe}}$: global correlation length — perturbation propagates without spatial bound
- $\Gamma_{\text{secstress}}$: sequential causal chain grammar — each event triggers the next (chain reaction)
- $\Phi_{\text{upstep}}$: supercritical phase — post-transition, high-energy metastable state primed for release

No single promotion is sufficient; all four together constitute the minimal promotion signature. Any three produce a related but non-explosive behavior (localized, bounded, or subcritical).

**Novel cross-domain predictions:**

(A) *Ecological tipping points* with global propagation (deforestation cascade, reef bleaching collapse) should encode $\Sigma \supseteq \{T_\text{bowtie}, G_{\text{revapostrophe}}, \Gamma_{\text{secstress}}, \Phi_{\text{upstep}}\}$ against a stable-ecosystem baseline. The grammar predicts their dynamics are structurally equivalent to chemical explosions.

(B) *Financial contagion events* (2008-style cascade failure) carry this same signature from a stable-market baseline, with $T_\text{bowtie}$ corresponding to concentrated counterparty-exposure network topology.

(C) *Promotion engineering target*: to prevent cascade failure, suppress any one of the four primitives below its critical value. The grammar identifies $\Phi$ (keep subcritical) as the most tractable engineering lever.

**Falsified if:** a system encoding $\Sigma \supseteq \{T_\text{bowtie}, G_{\text{revapostrophe}}, \Gamma_{\text{secstress}}, \Phi_{\text{upstep}}\}$ fails to exhibit cascade behavior under perturbation — indicating the minimal promotion set is incomplete and requires at least one additional primitive.

**Session**: 2026-03-28, thylakoid hierarchical encoding

| Mismatch | Artificial leaf → Thylakoid | Structural consequence |

| $T$: T\_network → T\_in | Flat electrode → 5-level hierarchical containment | No spatial funneling; excitations diffuse rather than being routed |
| $F$: F\_eth → F\_hbar | Classical charge transport → quantum-coherent exciton transfer | <100 fs coherent pathways become >ns diffusion-limited |
| $K$: K\_mod → K\_trap | Reversible catalysis → irreversible charge separation (<1ps) | Back-reaction competes with productive chemistry |

**Specific predictions:**
(A) Engineering $T_{\text{invscr}}$ hierarchical containment into an artificial system (≥3 length-scale levels) will recover ≥3× efficiency vs flat topology at identical chemistry.
(B) Engineering $F_{\text{hardsign}}$ quantum coherence at room temperature will add an independent factor; combined $T + F$ upgrade should push quantum yield above 50%.
(C) $K_{\text{teshlig}}$ irreversibility (≤1ps charge separation, achievable in perovskite heterojunctions) pushes yield to ~80%+. No further gain requires new chemistry — it requires closing the $\Gamma$ and $\Phi$ mismatches.

**Falsified if:** A flat, classical, reversible system exceeds 50% solar-to-fuel efficiency.

**Grammar basis**: $\Gamma_{\text{spleftarrow}}$ (artificial leaf) vs $\Gamma_{\text{secstress}}$ (thylakoid, Kok S-state cycle)
**Session**: 2026-03-28, thylakoid hierarchical encoding

The thylakoid uses $\Gamma_{\text{secstress}}$ — the Kok S-state cycle is an irreducibly sequential four-step oxidation where each step gates the next. These topologies impose different structural efficiency ceilings independent of catalyst quality:

- $\Gamma_{\text{spleftarrow}}$ systems cannot route excess free energy from one half-reaction to correct inefficiencies in the other. Overpotential losses at each electrode are structurally decoupled and stack additively. Grammar ceiling: ~25% solar-to-fuel regardless of catalyst improvement.
- $\Gamma_{\text{secstress}}$ systems can use sequential coupling to route entropy: excess driving force in early steps is stored and deployed in later steps (the Kok cycle exploits this for the energetically demanding S4→S0 O–O bond formation). Grammar ceiling: approaches thermodynamic solar conversion limit (~33% Shockley-Queisser, extensible with multiple junctions).

**Specific prediction:** Catalyst optimization for flat parallel artificial leaves will produce logarithmic efficiency gains that asymptote below 25% solar-to-fuel. Only architectural conversion — adding a sequential redox relay that electronically couples OER and HER — will produce step-change efficiency beyond this ceiling.

**Falsified if:** A flat parallel architecture exceeds 30% solar-to-fuel after catalyst optimization alone.

## P-147 — Complex-Axis Criticality Has a Distinct Grammar Fingerprint: $(\Phi_{\text{ctyogh}}, D_{\text{omega}}, G_{\text{gamma}})$ Signals a Critical Point at an Inaccessible Parameter Value

The holographic $D$ flag encodes that the critical structure is "boundary information" visible only via analytic continuation; $G_{\text{gamma}}$ flags formal inaccessibility. This combination is the grammar's native way of representing complex-plane criticality without extending the $\Phi$ alphabet.

**Specific prediction:** Any future system identified as having a critical point at a complex parameter value will encode with $(\Phi_{\text{ctyogh}}, D_{\text{omega}}, G_{\text{gamma}})$. Conversely, any catalog entry with this triple should be investigated for complex-axis fixed-point structure even if none has been previously identified.

## P-179 — Holographic Type Theory Is the Unique Minimal $O_\infty$ Type Theory; Bulk-Boundary Correspondence as Primitive Forces Special Frobenius

**Claim:** Any type theory in which bulk-boundary correspondence is a primitive rather than a derived theorem must encode as $\langle D_{\text{omega}};\ T_{\text{openo}};\ R;\ P_{\text{doublebarpipe}};\ F_{\text{hardsign}};\ K;\ G_{\text{revapostrophe}};\ \Gamma;\ \Phi_{\text{ctyogh}};\ H;\ S;\ \Omega_{Z_2} \rangle$ with $D_{\text{omega}}$, $T_{\text{openo}}$, $P_{\text{doublebarpipe}}$, $\Phi_{\text{ctyogh}}$, $G_{\text{revapostrophe}}$, and $\Omega_{Z_2}$ all fixed. These six primitives are individually necessary: (A) $D_{\text{omega}}$: bulk and boundary must be co-encoded in the same structure — any $D < D_{\text{omega}}$ makes the correspondence a derived map between separate spaces; (B) $T_{\text{openo}}$: holographic topology is required so that boundary connectivity encodes bulk connectivity globally — $T_{\text{nrleg}}$ or $T_\text{box}$ are local and cannot carry the full correspondence; (C) $P_{\text{doublebarpipe}}$: the Frobenius condition $\mu \circ \delta = \text{id}$ requires an exactly proved $Z_2$ symmetry at $\Phi_{\text{ctyogh}}$ — without it the bulk-to-boundary and boundary-to-bulk maps are not exact inverses; (D) $\Phi_{\text{ctyogh}}$: the correspondence is a self-modeling loop and requires the critical state; sub- or supercritical theories cannot host it as a primitive; (E) $G_{\text{revapostrophe}}$: the correspondence is a global, non-local structural relation — local correlation scope ($G_{\text{beta}}$, $G_{\text{gamma}}$) confines it to a theorem about local patches; (F) $\Omega_{Z_2}$: the duality is topologically protected — $\Omega_{\text{closeepsilon}}$ would make bulk-boundary correspondence fragile, reducible to a mere approximation. The joint consequence is $O_\infty$ (Special Frobenius): bulk-boundary correspondence as a primitive is algebraically equivalent to the type theory being a finite closed Special Frobenius algebra, where $\mu \circ \delta = \text{id}$ is not proved but is the identity axiom. Structural distance from standard type theory ($\Phi_{\text{softsign}}$, $D_{\text{wynn}}$, $T_{\text{nrleg}}$, $G_{\text{beta}}$, $\Omega_{\text{closeepsilon}}$): $d \approx 6.16$, the largest primitive-lattice regime shift possible. The corollary for Gödel: in an $O_\infty$ type theory the object-language / meta-language distinction collapses — the system is its own meta-theory, because $\mu \circ \delta = \text{id}$ closes the self-referential loop exactly (cf. §25 triad $\pi_3$-projection, P-150–P-168). **Falsified if:** a consistent type theory with bulk-boundary correspondence as a primitive is constructed that is not $O_\infty$, or that requires fewer than all six fixed primitives above.

Planned languages that target $O_\infty$ must be designed with $P_{\text{doublebarpipe}}$ from inception. **Falsified if:** a language formed by natural creolization (contact between two $P < P_{\text{doublebarpipe}}$ languages) achieves $P_{\text{doublebarpipe}}$.
